IMAGE         ID             DISK USAGE   CONTENT SIZE
counter:1.0   3f1a...        245MB        62.9MB
Trap

docker build -t app:dev Dockerfile.dev treats Dockerfile.dev as the context folder: unable to prepare context: path "Dockerfile.dev" not found. A file goes after -f; the last argument is always a folder.

Dockerfile instructions and the layer cache

A Dockerfile is a list of steps. Most steps create a layer. Layers are read-only and shared: 100 containers from one image use the same layers on disk (copy-on-write); each container only adds its own thin writable layer.

InstructionWhat it does
FROM node:24-alpinethe base image: user space + Node runtime. Always first.
WORKDIR /app"cd" for all later steps (created if missing). Relative paths like ./ mean /app/.
COPY package.json package-lock.json ./copy files from the build context into the image
RUN npm ci --omit=devruns at build time; its result is saved as a layer
ENV MODE=devenvironment variable baked into the image (override at run with -e)
EXPOSE 3000documents the port. It does not publish it. Only -p on docker run publishes.
CMD ["node", "server.js"]the default command when a container starts. Not run at build time. Replaced by any command typed after the image name in docker run.

The cache rule: a changed input invalidates that step and every step after it. So put what changes rarely (dependency manifests and the install) before what changes often (your source).

# GOOD ORDER (the slide / Quiz 2 Q15 answer)
FROM node:24-alpine
WORKDIR /app
COPY package.json package-lock.json ./   # 1. only the manifests
RUN npm ci                               # 2. install; cached while the manifests don't change
COPY . .                                 # 3. source last: editing server.js only reruns from here
EXPOSE 3000
CMD ["node", "server.js"]
# after editing only server.js:
$ docker build -t shop-api:1.0 .
#6 [2/5] WORKDIR /app
#6 CACHED
#7 [3/5] COPY package.json package-lock.json ./
#7 CACHED
#8 [4/5] RUN npm ci
#8 CACHED
#9 [5/5] COPY . .                                  # reruns (its input changed)
Why

RUN npm ci before COPY package*.json fails: when the RUN step executes, /app is empty, so there is no lockfile. COPY . . before RUN npm ci builds, but every server.js edit changes the input of COPY . ., so npm ci reruns every time (slow). npm ci installs exactly what package-lock.json pins, and refuses to run without it.

.dockerignore lists what to leave out of the build context, like .gitignore. Typical: node_modules, .git, .env, data. Keeps secrets out of the image, keeps the context small, and stops your laptop's node_modules from overwriting the one built inside the image by COPY . ..

npm error The `npm ci` command can only install with an existing package-lock.json or
npm error npm-shrinkwrap.json with lockfileVersion >= 1.
ERROR: failed to build: failed to solve: process "/bin/sh -c npm ci" did not complete successfully: exit code: 1

Means: the lockfile isn't in the image when RUN npm ci runs (wrong step order, or the lockfile is missing from the folder / excluded by .dockerignore).

Fix: COPY package.json package-lock.json ./ before RUN npm ci.

docker run: options before the image, command after

docker run = create a new container from an image and start it. Each run makes a new container with a fresh, empty writable layer.

docker run -d -p 8080:3000 -v counter-data:/data --name counter counter:1.0
  1. create + start a new container
  2. -d = detached: run in the background, print the container id, give the terminal back
  3. -p HOST:CONTAINER = publish: traffic to host port 8080 goes to container port 3000
  4. -v VOLUME:/path = mount the named volume counter-data at /data inside
  5. --name = a name to use instead of the id (must be unique among all containers)
  6. the image. Everything after this word is the command to run inside the container, replacing CMD.
Trap: options after the image

docker run -d counter:1.0 -p 8080:3000 -v counter-data:/data does not publish anything. -p 8080:3000 -v ... becomes the container's command. With node images it runs node -p 8080:3000 -v ..., prints the Node version and exits: docker ps is empty, docker ps -a says Exited (0), docker logs shows v24.x.x. With alpine: exec: "-p": executable file not found in $PATH. Rule: docker run [options] IMAGE [command].

OptionWhat it does / why
-dbackground. Without it your terminal shows the app's output; Ctrl+C stops the app.
-p 8080:3000publish on every host interface (PORTS shows 0.0.0.0:8080->3000/tcp). Order: host first.
-p 127.0.0.1:8080:3000publish only on the host's loopback: reachable from this machine only, not from the network
-p 127.0.0.1::3000let Docker pick a free host port (see it with docker port NAME)
--name webname the container
-e KEY=valueset an environment variable inside (config, not baked in the image)
-v data:/dataleft side is a name (no /) = named volume
-v "$(pwd)"/data:/dataleft side is a path = bind mount of a host folder
--mount type=volume,src=data,dst=/datalong, explicit form of the named volume (no spaces after commas!)
--mount type=bind,src="$(pwd)"/data,dst=/datalong form of a bind mount (path must exist)
--rmdelete the container automatically when it exits (one-shot tasks)
-it IMAGE sh-i keep input open + -t a terminal: an interactive shell. exit leaves (and stops the container, since sh is PID 1).
--cpus=0.5cgroup CPU cap: half a CPU. Over it = throttled (keeps running, slower).
--memory=512mcgroup memory cap. Over it = the kernel may kill the process. Needs a unit (m, g).
$ docker run --rm alpine:3.24 echo "Hello from a pulled image"
Hello from a pulled image                     # command after the image replaced CMD; --rm cleaned up
$ docker run --rm -it --name pid1 alpine:3.24 sh
/ # ps
PID   USER     TIME  COMMAND
    1 root      0:00 sh                      # PID 1 inside its own PID namespace
/ # exit
Trap: redirects run on the host

docker run --rm -v notes-data:/data alpine:3.24 echo hi > /data/x.txt: your host shell does the > before Docker even starts: bash: /data/x.txt: No such file or directory. Put it inside: ... alpine:3.24 sh -c 'echo hi > /data/x.txt'.

Ports: ps, EXPOSE vs -p, 0.0.0.0 vs 127.0.0.1, curl

A request from your laptop goes: curl -> host port -> published-port rule -> container's own IP : container port -> the server, which must be listening there.

$ docker ps
CONTAINER ID   IMAGE         STATUS         PORTS                                          NAMES
8dbd4e173097   counter:1.0   Up 2 minutes   0.0.0.0:8080->3000/tcp, [::]:8080->3000/tcp    counter
1b2c3d4e5f60   t3demo:1      Up 5 seconds   3000/tcp                                       t3web
9f8e7d6c5b4a   greeter:1.0   Up 1 minute    127.0.0.1:8081->3000/tcp                       greeter-web
$ curl http://localhost:8080/
count=1
PORTS columnMeaning
3000/tcpEXPOSEd only. Nothing is published. curl from the host can't reach it. (The [::] twin is the same rule for IPv6.)
0.0.0.0:8080->3000/tcppublished: host port 8080 on all interfaces -> container 3000
127.0.0.1:8081->3000/tcppublished on the host loopback only
(empty)container not running, or no ports at all. Check docker ps -a.
Trap: the server listens on 127.0.0.1 inside the container

Quiz 2 Q5. docker ps looks perfect (0.0.0.0:8080->3000/tcp) but curl says (52) Empty reply from server or Connection reset. Inside a container, 127.0.0.1 is the container's own loopback (its own network namespace). Forwarded traffic arrives on the container's network address, so a server bound to 127.0.0.1 never sees it. Fix the app to listen on 0.0.0.0, rebuild, and recreate the container. Adding EXPOSE changes nothing.

Tip

Two different "127.0.0.1"s: -p 127.0.0.1:8080:3000 limits the host side (good, safe). listen(3000, "127.0.0.1") in the app limits the container side (bad: unreachable). Check what the app says with docker logs NAME.

Storage: writable layer, volumes, bind mounts, what survives

Files a container writes go to its private writable layer, unless the path is a mount. The writable layer lives exactly as long as the container. A named volume lives until you docker volume rm it.

ActionWritable layer (e.g. /tmp/session.log)Named volume (e.g. /data/count.txt)
docker stop then docker startkept (same container)kept
docker restartkept (same container)kept
docker rm + a new docker rungone (new container, empty layer)kept: the new container sees it again
docker volume rm-gone (refused while any container, even stopped, uses it)
CommandWhat it does
docker volume create counter-datamake a named volume
docker volume lslist volumes
docker volume inspect counter-datadetails; Mountpoint = where Docker keeps it on the host (/var/lib/docker/volumes/...)
docker volume rm counter-datadelete it and its data, permanently
docker run --rm -v counter-data:/data alpine:3.24 cat /data/count.txtpeek into a volume with a throw-away container
Why named volume vs bind mount

Named volume: Docker manages where it lives; lifecycle separate from containers; use for app data (databases, counters). Bind mount: a specific host folder mapped in; use in development when the host path itself matters (live-edit code). -v name:/data vs -v /abs/path:/data: a leading / (or ./, $(pwd)) silently switches to a bind mount.

Look inside: ps -a, logs, exec, top, inspect, stop/rm

CommandWhat it does
docker ps / docker ps -arunning containers / all (incl. Exited and Created). Always check -a when something "disappeared".
docker ps --format '{{.Names}} {{.Image}} {{.Ports}}'only the columns you want
docker ps --filter ancestor=counter:1.0only containers from that image (also name=, volume=, status=exited)
docker port webwhich host port maps to which container port
docker logs web / docker logs -f webwhat the app printed (stdout/stderr). -f = follow live; Ctrl+C stops following, not the container.
docker exec web cat /data/count.txtrun a command inside a running container
docker exec -it web sha shell inside; exit leaves it, the container keeps running (sh is not PID 1 here)
docker top webthe container's processes with their host PIDs
docker inspect webeverything as JSON (image, mounts, ports, env, limits, state)
docker inspect -f '{{.State.Status}}' webone field. Others: {{.Config.Image}}, {{.State.Pid}}, {{json .Mounts}}, {{json .Config.Env}}, {{.HostConfig.Memory}}
docker stop web / docker start webstop the process (container kept, name + port bindings kept) / start the same container again
docker rm web / docker rm -f webdelete a stopped container / stop and delete in one go. Frees the name.
$ docker exec notes-api ps
PID   USER     TIME  COMMAND
    1 root      0:00 node server.js          # PID 1 inside (PID namespace)
$ docker top notes-api
UID    PID      PPID     C   STIME   TTY   TIME       CMD
root   809000   808976   0   22:11   ?     00:00:00   node server.js   # same process, host PID
$ docker inspect -f '{{.State.Pid}}' notes-api
809000
Tip

A container lives as long as its PID 1. If docker ps doesn't show your container, run docker ps -a and docker logs NAME: the main process exited (crash, wrong command, or options typed after the image).

Common errors, decoded

ERROR: docker: 'docker buildx build' requires 1 argument

Means: no build context given.

Fix: add . at the end: docker build -t counter:1.0 .

docker: Error response from daemon: ... Bind for 0.0.0.0:8080 failed: port is already allocated

Means: another running container already publishes host port 8080. Docker has already created your new container (status Created) before failing.

Fix: docker ps to find who holds 8080; docker stop or docker rm -f it (or use another host port if allowed); then docker rm your half-made container and run again.

docker: Error response from daemon: Conflict. The container name "/orders" is already in use by container "8da5...". You have to remove (or rename) that container to be able to reuse that name.

Means: a container with that name exists, maybe stopped or Created (docker ps -a).

Fix: docker rm orders (or rm -f if running), then run again. docker start orders would restart the old container, not a new one from a new image.

Unable to find image 'countr:1.0' locally
docker: Error response from daemon: pull access denied for countr, repository does not exist or may require 'docker login'

Means: no local image with that exact name:tag, so Docker tried to download it from Docker Hub and it doesn't exist there either. Usually a typo, a wrong tag, or you never built it.

Fix: docker image ls, use the exact name:tag (or build it first).

ERROR: failed to build: invalid tag "Counter:1.0": repository name must be lowercase

Means: image names must be lowercase (tags may have letters, digits, . _ -).

Fix: -t counter:1.0.

node: bad option: --name        (in docker logs; container Exited)
exec: "--name": executable file not found in $PATH

Means: you put options after the image name; they became the command.

Fix: docker rm it, then put every option before the image.

Error response from daemon: remove counter-data: volume is in use - [8dbd4e17...]
Error response from daemon: conflict: unable to delete counter:1.0 (must be forced) - image is being used by stopped container 8dbd4e17

Means: a container (even a stopped one) still uses it.

Fix: docker rm -f the container first, then remove the volume / image.

invalid argument "type=volume," for "--mount" flag: invalid field '' must be a key=value pair

Means: spaces after commas in --mount split it into separate words.

Fix: --mount type=volume,src=data,dst=/data with no spaces.

curl: (7) Failed to connect to localhost port 8080: Connection refused
curl: (52) Empty reply from server

Means: (7) nothing published on that host port (no -p, wrong port, or container not running). (52)/(56) the port is published but the app inside listens on 127.0.0.1 or crashed.

Fix: check docker ps PORTS, then docker logs.

Quiz-style Quiz 2 Q14: build, volume, run the counter

Your folder has a Dockerfile for a small counter service. No image, volume or container exists yet. Build it as counter:1.0, create volume counter-data, start a container in the background that publishes host 8080 to container 3000 and mounts counter-data at /data. Don't stop or remove it.

Show answer
$ docker build -t counter:1.0 .
$ docker volume create counter-data
$ docker run -d --name counter -p 8080:3000 -v counter-data:/data counter:1.0
$ docker ps
... Up 2 seconds   0.0.0.0:8080->3000/tcp, [::]:8080->3000/tcp   counter
$ curl http://localhost:8080/
count=1

Dot = build context. All options before the image. 8080:3000 = host:container. The name counter-data without a slash = named volume.

Quiz-style Quiz 2 Q15: reorder the shop-api Dockerfile

The Dockerfile is FROM / WORKDIR /app / RUN npm ci / COPY package.json package-lock.json ./ / COPY . . / EXPOSE 3000 / CMD. The build fails. Only reorder lines so it builds and a server.js-only change doesn't rerun npm ci. Build shop-api:1.0. Don't run a container.

Show answer
FROM node:24-slim
WORKDIR /app
COPY package.json package-lock.json ./
RUN npm ci
COPY . .
EXPOSE 3000
CMD ["node", "server.js"]
$ docker build -t shop-api:1.0 .

npm ci needs the lockfile in the image first. Source is copied last so its changes only invalidate COPY . . and later steps.

Drill Which steps rerun?

Dockerfile: FROM node:24-alpine, WORKDIR /app, COPY package.json package-lock.json ./, RUN npm ci --omit=dev, COPY server.js ./, CMD ["node","server.js"]. (a) You edit server.js. (b) You add a dependency (package.json + lockfile change). What reruns in each case?

Show answer

(a) COPY server.js ./ and everything after it (CMD only records metadata). (b) COPY package.json package-lock.json ./, RUN npm ci, COPY server.js and the rest: a changed input invalidates that step and every step after it. FROM and WORKDIR are cached in both.

Quiz-style What is the count after rm?

docker run -d --name c -v t3data:/data counter:1.0. Three requests: count.txt = 3, and each request also writes a line to /tmp/session.log. You docker rm -f c and run the same command again, then send one request. Count? Old log lines? And what if you had done docker stop c + docker start c instead?

Show answer

After rm + run: count=4 (the volume survived), old log lines are gone (/tmp was in the deleted writable layer). After stop + start: count=4 and the log lines are still there (same container, same writable layer).

Twist Up, published, still unreachable

docker run -d -p 8080:3000 --name web app:1.0. docker ps shows 0.0.0.0:8080->3000/tcp, but curl localhost:8080 gets an empty reply. docker logs web says listening on 127.0.0.1:3000. Fix it.

Show answer

In the Files panel open server.js, change the listen line to .listen(3000, "0.0.0.0") and save with Ctrl+S. Then:

$ docker build -t app:1.1 .
$ docker rm -f web
$ docker run -d -p 8080:3000 --name web app:1.1
$ curl localhost:8080

127.0.0.1 inside the container is the container's own loopback. A new image needs a new container: restart would reuse the old image.

Twist Port taken and name taken

orders-old (v1) is running on 8090. A stopped container named orders exists. Run orders:2.0 as orders on 8090 -> 3000. Keep orders-old (stopped) for its logs.

Show answer
$ docker stop orders-old           # frees port 8090, keeps container + logs
$ docker rm orders                  # frees the name
$ docker run -d --name orders -p 8090:3000 orders:2.0

Ports belong to running containers; names belong to all containers. If you run first, you get Conflict ... name already in use, then port is already allocated (which leaves a Created container you must rm).

Drill Namespaces or cgroups?

docker run -d --cpus=0.5 --memory=512m --name api api:1.0. (a) Inside, what PID does the main process have? (b) It wants 2 CPUs. (c) It allocates 1 GB. (d) What hides the host's other processes?

Show answer

(a) PID 1 inside (PID namespace); another PID on the host (docker top api). (b) Throttled: keeps running, slower. (c) The kernel may kill it (OOM), not just slow it. (d) Namespaces (what you see), not cgroups (how much you use).

Quiz-style The password is in the image

A teammate's Dockerfile has ENV DB_PASSWORD=Summer2024! and COPY . ., and there is no .dockerignore. The project folder has a .env with DB_HOST and DB_PASSWORD. They built pay:1.0. (a) Show two ways to read the password from the image. (b) Fix the build and build pay:1.1. (c) Run it as pay on host 8080 -> 3000 with the settings from .env, using one flag. (d) Remove the leaky tag.

Show answer
$ docker history --no-trunc pay:1.0 | grep ENV      # ENV DB_PASSWORD=Summer2024!
$ docker run --rm pay:1.0 cat /app/.env              # COPY . . copied .env in

In the Files panel open Dockerfile, delete the two ENV DB_... lines, save with Ctrl+S. Click + New file, type .dockerignore, make it read:

.env
node_modules
.git

Save with Ctrl+S. Then in the terminal:

$ docker build -t pay:1.1 .
$ docker run -d --name pay -p 8080:3000 --env-file .env pay:1.1
$ docker image rm pay:1.0

ENV (and ARG) values are recorded in the image metadata and in docker history. COPY . . copies everything in the build context that .dockerignore doesn't exclude. A later RUN rm .env doesn't help: the file stays in the earlier layer. Config and secrets go in the environment at run time (--env-file or -e), so one image runs anywhere. Lab 10.

Twist web can't reach api on localhost

api runs with -p 8119:3000. web runs with -e API_URL=http://localhost:8119 -p 8111:3000. From your laptop, curl localhost:8119 works, but web logs ECONNREFUSED. Make web reach api by name, and make api internal only (no host port).

Show answer
$ docker network create appnet
$ docker rm -f api web
$ docker run -d --name api --network appnet api:1.0
$ docker run -d --name web --network appnet -p 8111:3000 -e API_URL=http://api:3000 web:1.0
$ docker exec web wget -qO- http://api:3000/stock     # debug from inside

Inside a container, localhost is that container (its own network namespace). 8119 is a host port; container-to-container traffic uses the container port. Names resolve only on a user-defined network, not on the default bridge. Env, ports and networks are fixed at create time, so recreate (restart keeps the old ones). Lab 11.

Twist It exits right after docker run

docker run -d --name report -p 8112:3000 report:1.0 prints an id, but docker ps is empty. The app needs /config/settings.json, which ops keeps on the host in ./config/ and wants to edit without rebuilding. (a) How do you find the reason and the exit code? (b) Run it correctly, config read-only. (c) Ops changes the title in the file. What must you do so the app shows it?

Show answer
$ docker ps -a                                   # report  Exited (3)
$ docker logs report                             # FATAL: cannot read /config/settings.json (ENOENT)
$ docker inspect -f '{{.State.ExitCode}}' report  # 3
$ docker rm report
$ docker run -d --name report -p 8112:3000 -v "$(pwd)/config":/config:ro report:1.0
# (c) after editing config/settings.json in the Files panel (it is the host file) and saving:
$ docker restart report

-d only says the container started; the main process exiting ends it. Logs survive on stopped containers. A left side starting with / is a bind mount (a host folder you choose); -v config:/config would create an empty named volume instead. The container sees the edited file immediately, but this app reads it only at start, so restart. Mounts can't be added to an existing container: rm + run. Lab 12.

Twist Prove the cache, then retag

The Dockerfile is FROM / WORKDIR /app / COPY . . / RUN npm ci / CMD, and the folder has a 30 MB logs/ and a laptop node_modules/. Make server.js edits skip npm ci (you may add lines), keep the junk out, build shop:1.2 saving the plain build output to build.log (and on screen), point shop:latest at 1.2 without rebuilding, and delete shop:1.0.

Show answer

In the Files panel open Dockerfile, make it read:

FROM node:24-alpine
WORKDIR /app
COPY package.json package-lock.json ./
RUN npm ci --omit=dev
COPY . .
CMD ["node", "server.js"]

Save with Ctrl+S. Click + New file, type .dockerignore, make it read:

logs
node_modules
.git
.env

Save with Ctrl+S. Then in the terminal:

$ docker build --progress=plain -t shop:1.2 . 2>&1 | tee build.log
#8 [4/5] RUN npm ci --omit=dev
#8 CACHED
$ docker tag shop:1.2 shop:latest
$ docker image rm shop:1.0

Build output goes to stderr, so without 2>&1 the log file stays empty. A tag is just a name pointing at an image ID: docker tag SOURCE NEW, no copy, same IMAGE ID. image rm only untags while other tags still point at that image. Lab 13.

Practice labs

Cheat sheet

  • docker build -t name:tag . build; the dot = build context (required)
  • docker build -t name:tag -f Other.Dockerfile . another Dockerfile, still the dot
  • docker image ls / docker image rm name:tag / docker history name:tag
  • docker run -d --name n -p 8080:3000 -v vol:/data -e K=v image:tag options BEFORE the image
  • -p 127.0.0.1:8080:3000 host-only publish; order is HOST:CONTAINER
  • --mount type=volume,src=vol,dst=/data long form (no spaces)
  • docker run --rm -it image sh throw-away interactive shell
  • --cpus=0.5 --memory=512m cgroup limits: CPU throttles, memory may kill
  • docker ps / docker ps -a running / all; read the PORTS column
  • docker logs -f n / docker exec n cmd / docker exec -it n sh
  • docker top n / docker inspect -f '{{.State.Pid}}' n host view of PID 1
  • docker stop n + docker start n same container, data kept
  • docker rm -f n container + writable layer gone; volumes stay
  • docker volume create|ls|inspect|rm vol
  • EXPOSE documents only; -p publishes; app must listen on 0.0.0.0
  • Dockerfile order: FROM, WORKDIR, COPY package*.json ./, RUN npm ci, COPY . ., EXPOSE, CMD
  • curl http://localhost:8080/ test from the host
  • docker run --env-file .env ... config/secrets at run time, never ENV in the Dockerfile
  • docker network create net + --network net containers reach each other by name on the container port
  • -v "$(pwd)/config":/config:ro read-only bind mount of a host folder
  • docker tag src:1.2 src:latest another name, same image ID
Topic 5

Testing with pytest, the failing-test-then-fix workflow, and reading CI config

Run and select tests, write boundary tests that catch real bugs, commit "failing test" then "fix" in exactly the right order, undo a wrong commit, and read a GitHub Actions workflow.

Mental model

A test is a claim you can run: set up (Arrange), call the code (Act), check the result (Assert). No assert = no claim. A bug fix is two commits: commit 1 adds a test that is red on the old code (proof the bug exists), commit 2 is the smallest change that makes it green. The grader looks at those commits by position: HEAD~1 = your test commit, HEAD = your fix. CI just reruns the same tests on a clean machine; branch protection is what turns a red CI run into a blocked merge.

The vocabulary (what the MCQs ask)

These words come straight from the T2L2 slides. Know each one in one sentence.

WordMeaning
Arrange-Act-AssertThe shape of every test: set up the world, do the thing, check the claim.
Test case / suite / runnerOne scenario / a named group of cases / the program that runs them and reports (pytest).
AssertionStates the expected outcome and fails loudly if wrong. Code without one only proves "it did not crash".
Normal / boundary / invalidRule "at least 8 chars": normal = 12 chars, boundary = 7 (just below) and 8 (exactly at), invalid = empty / missing. Most bugs live at the boundary.
Unit testOne function or small module, no real network or database. Fast. E.g. password validation, tax calculation.
Integration testSeveral real parts together: route + validation + database. E.g. duplicate-email check against a real table; POST /api/login returns an error.
End-to-end (E2E)A browser drives a real user journey (sign up then sign in). Slowest, most brittle; keep only a few for critical paths.
Testing pyramidMany unit, some integration, few E2E. A budget, not a law.
Regression testA test written for a bug you fixed, so the bug can't silently come back.
CoverageWhich lines / branches / functions ran. It measures execution, not whether anything was checked.
StubReturns a fixed, canned value in place of a real dependency.
FakeA working but simplified implementation, e.g. an in-memory database.
MockRecords how it was called, so the test asserts on the interaction ("save was called"), not the result.
Mutation testingA tool makes a small change (e.g. >= to >) and reruns tests. A surviving mutant = your tests can't tell broken code from right code.
CIEvery proposed change is automatically built and tested on a clean machine. CI validates; it does not deploy (that's CD).
Trap

"100% coverage" does not mean the code is tested. A test that calls signup and asserts True covers every line and checks nothing. Quiz 2 Q2: a Mock test proves save was called, not that the response was 201; an in-memory fake + asserting 201 and the stored user checks the behaviour.

Running pytest and reading its output

pytest looks in the current folder (and subfolders) for files named test_*.py or *_test.py, and inside them runs every function whose name starts with test_. Always run it from the project folder (where the files are).

pytest -v -k "password and not short" test_users.py
  1. the test runner
  2. -v = verbose: one line per test with PASSED/FAILED
  3. -k = keep only tests whose name matches this expression (and / or / not; quote it)
  4. optional: which file (or folder) to run; default is everything
CommandWhat it does
pytestrun all tests; one character per test: . pass, F fail, E error (crash before/outside the assert, e.g. import error)
pytest -qquiet: less header, short summary
pytest -vverbose: full name + result for every test
pytest -xstop at the first failure (fast feedback)
pytest -k emailonly tests with "email" in the name (case-insensitive)
pytest test_validate.pyonly that file
pytest test_validate.py::test_accepts_exactly_eight_charactersonly that one test (its "node id", copy it from the FAILED line)
pytest -sshow print() output (normally hidden for passing tests)
pytest --lfrerun only the tests that failed last time
echo $?exit code of the LAST command: run it straight after pytest
pytest exit codeMeaning
0all selected tests passed
1some tests failed (this is what CI sees as "red")
2interrupted (Ctrl+C, or errors while collecting, e.g. syntax error in a test file)
4usage error, e.g. file or directory not found
5no tests collected (wrong file names, or -k matched nothing)
$ pytest
collected 3 items

test_validate.py ..F                                                     [100%]

=================================== FAILURES ===================================
____________________ test_accepts_exactly_eight_characters _____________________

    def test_accepts_exactly_eight_characters():
>       assert is_valid_length("abcdefgh") is True
E       AssertionError: assert False is True
E        +  where False = is_valid_length('abcdefgh')

test_validate.py:13: AssertionError
=========================== short test summary info ============================
FAILED test_validate.py::test_accepts_exactly_eight_characters - AssertionErr...
========================= 1 failed, 2 passed in 0.05s ==========================
$ echo $?
1
# read it bottom-up: counts -> which test -> the E lines: the call returned False, we expected True
Tip

In assert X == Y pytest prints the evaluated values in the same order: assert 190 == 180 means the code gave 190 and the test expected 180. The + where line tells you which call produced the value.

ERROR: file or directory not found: and

Means: you wrote pytest -k password and not short without quotes; the shell split it and pytest thinks and is a file.

Fix: pytest -k "password and not short"

ERROR: file or directory not found: test_password_too_short

Means: a bare test name is treated as a path.

Fix: pytest test_users.py::test_password_too_short or pytest -k test_password_too_short.

ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'validate'

Means: pytest was run from the wrong folder, or the import name doesn't match the file name.

Fix: cd into the folder that holds validate.py and run pytest there. In tests: from validate import is_valid_length (file name without .py).

no tests ran in 0.00s

Means: exit code 5: nothing collected. The file isn't named test_*.py, the function doesn't start with test_, or -k matched nothing.

Fix: rename, or check the -k word.

Writing tests: assert, boundaries, pytest.raises

A pytest test is just a function named test_... that uses plain assert. Name it like a specification: the name should say what broke when it goes red.

# test_validate.py
import pytest
from validate import is_valid_length

def test_accepts_exactly_eight_characters():   # boundary, at the limit
    # Arrange
    password = "abcdefgh"
    # Act
    result = is_valid_length(password)
    # Assert
    assert result is True

def test_rejects_seven_characters():           # boundary, one below
    assert is_valid_length("abcdefg") is False

def test_none_is_rejected():                   # invalid input -> must raise
    with pytest.raises(TypeError):
        is_valid_length(None)
with pytest.raises(ValueError): is_adult(-1)
  1. passes only if the indented block raises ValueError (or a subclass); otherwise the test fails with DID NOT RAISE
  2. the call that should blow up goes inside the block

Which values to test for a rule "at least N": N-1 (last bad), N (first good), one normal value, one invalid input. 3 and 20 are useless for the edge: they give the same answer for >= 8, > 8 and >= 7.

Bug (mutant) in the codeTest that catches (kills) it
len(pw) > 8 instead of >= 8exactly 8 chars must be accepted
len(pw) >= 7exactly 7 chars must be rejected
len(pw) >= 9exactly 8 chars must be accepted
Trap

A test with no assert always passes unless the code crashes:

def test_ten_percent_off_200():
    result = apply_discount(200, 10)
    print("result is", result)      # green, even though result is 190, not 180

Also weak: assert result (any non-zero number passes) and assert status != 201 (a 500 passes). Assert the exact value.

Trap

Two test functions with the same name in one file: Python keeps only the last one, so the first test silently disappears. pytest -v shows the real list.

Failed: DID NOT RAISE <class 'ValueError'>

Means: the code inside with pytest.raises(...) returned normally.

Fix: either the code has a bug (good, you found it) or you put the wrong call / wrong exception type in the block.

NameError: name 'pytest' is not defined

Fix: add import pytest at the top of the test file (only needed for pytest.raises etc.; plain asserts don't need it).

Failing test, then fix: exactly two commits

This is quiz 2 Q16 (5 marks) and Milestone 1. The checks look at commit positions: HEAD~1 must contain the new test and the old code; HEAD has the fix. So the order and the "only one file per commit" rule matter.

$ cat validate.py                     # find the bug first: len(password) > 8
$ pytest                              # 2 passed: existing tests miss the edge
# Files panel: open test_validate.py, add test_accepts_exactly_eight_characters, Ctrl+S
$ pytest                              # 1 failed, 2 passed  -> RED is what you want now
$ git status --short
 M test_validate.py
$ git add test_validate.py            # ONLY the test file
$ git commit -m "Add failing test for 8-character password"
# Files panel: open validate.py, > 8 becomes >= 8, Ctrl+S
$ pytest                              # 3 passed -> GREEN
$ git add validate.py
$ git commit -m "Fix password length check to accept 8 characters"
$ git log --oneline
c3d4e5f (HEAD -> main) Fix password length check to accept 8 characters
a1b2c3d Add failing test for 8-character password
b8b4933 Add password length check

Check yourself the way the grader does:

CommandWhat it shows
git log --onelinenumber of commits and their order (newest on top)
git show --stat HEAD~1which files the test commit changed (should be only the test file)
git show HEAD~1:validate.pythe file as it was in that commit (must still have the bug)
git log -1 --format=%s HEAD~1that commit's message (must contain "failing test")
git diff HEAD~1 HEADwhat the fix commit changed
Trap

git add . or git commit -am "..." after you already touched validate.py puts the fix into the "failing test" commit. Then HEAD~1:validate.py is no longer the original and the test never failed in history. Always git status before committing and add the one file by name.

Why

The red commit is evidence the bug existed and that your test can see it. If you fix first, you never saw the test fail, so you don't know it can fail. Six months later, the test is what stops the bug coming back (regression).

Tip

The labs ignore __pycache__/ and .pytest_cache/ with a .gitignore. In the quiz, if git status shows those folders as untracked, just don't add them: add your file by name.

Undo: reset --soft, --amend, and what not to use

Made an extra commit, or committed the wrong thing? Nothing pushed yet = you can safely rewrite your local history.

git reset --soft HEAD~1
  1. move the current branch pointer
  2. --soft = keep the changes from the undone commit, still staged
  3. to one commit before HEAD (the commit is removed from the branch)
CommandCommitYour changes end up
git reset --soft HEAD~1removedstaged (ready to commit again)
git reset HEAD~1 (= --mixed)removedin the files, unstaged
git reset --hard HEAD~1removeddeleted - careful
git revert HEADkept, plus a NEW commit that undoes it-- (adds a commit: wrong when the count must be exact)
git commit --amend -m "msg"last commit replaced (new ID)whatever is staged is added into it
git restore --staged f--f unstaged, content kept
git restore f--f's unstaged edits thrown away
# Q16 gone wrong: you committed a typo fix as a 3rd commit. Fold it into the fix commit:
$ git reset --soft HEAD~1         # 3rd commit gone, its change is staged
$ git commit --amend --no-edit    # add the staged change into the fix commit, keep its message
$ git log --oneline               # back to exactly 2 new commits

# Wrong message on the last commit (e.g. forgot "failing test"):
$ git commit --amend -m "Add failing test for 8-character password"
Trap

--amend always changes the last commit. If you already made the fix commit, amending to fix the test commit's message would rewrite the fix instead. Then: git reset --soft HEAD~1 (fix changes now staged), git stash, amend the test commit, git stash pop, commit the fix again. Easier: check the message before the second commit. git commit with no -m (and --amend without -m/--no-edit) opens an editor; in nano save with Ctrl+O, Enter, Ctrl+X.

Trap

Never amend or reset commits you already pushed to a shared branch: everyone else's history stops matching. The quiz says "Don't push", so local rewriting is fine.

Coverage (concept + command)

Coverage needs the pytest-cov plugin. It is not installed on this machine, so the labs don't use it; know the command and how to read it.

$ pip install pytest-cov
$ pytest --cov=. --cov-report=term-missing
Name          Stmts   Miss  Cover   Missing
-------------------------------------------
validate.py       2      0   100%
# 100% = every line RAN during the tests. It says nothing about whether the result was asserted.
# --cov-branch adds branch coverage (were both sides of each if taken?)
Why

The slides' example: a signup test that posts a user and asserts True gives 100% coverage of the route, but the route could return 500 and the test would still pass. Mutation testing is the stronger signal: it checks that the tests would notice a change.

Reading a GitHub Actions workflow

A workflow is a YAML file in .github/workflows/. GitHub runs it on a fresh, clean machine ("runner") when the events under on: happen. Any step that exits non-zero (pytest exit code 1) fails the job: a red X.

name: CI
on:                            # WHEN to run
  push:
    branches: [main]           # after commits land on main
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]           # on every PR that targets main -> the PR shows a check
jobs:
  test:                        # job id - this name shows up as the status check
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest     # fresh VM each run
    steps:                     # run in order; first failure stops the job
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4          # get the repo code
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5      # install a runtime
        with:
          python-version: "3.12"
      - run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - run: pytest -q                     # exit code 1 -> job red
KeyMeaning
on: push: branches: [main]runs only after code is already on main. Too late to stop a bad merge.
on: pull_request: branches: [main]runs on PRs into main, so reviewers see green/red before merging
jobs: / steps:jobs run in parallel on separate machines; steps run in order inside one job
uses: vs run:use a ready-made action / run a shell command
continue-on-error: truea failing step counts as success: hides red tests. Remove it.
services:a throw-away container (e.g. test database) for this job only; same level as steps:
env:environment variables for a step, e.g. a test-only DATABASE_URL
Trap

Required status checks are a GitHub setting, not YAML. Repo Settings -> Branches (branch protection rule) or Rules -> Rulesets -> "Require status checks to pass" -> pick the job (e.g. test). Quiz 2 Q3: to block merging a PR with failing tests you need BOTH the pull_request trigger (so the check runs on the PR) AND the required check (so red blocks the merge). Without the trigger, a required check never reports and every PR waits. Without the rule, red is only a warning. continue-on-error and "ask people in the README" are wrong answers.

Trap

YAML indentation is spaces only, and siblings must line up. pull_request: indented under push: becomes a setting of push, not a second trigger. A tab character = parse error. Quick local syntax check: python3 -c "import yaml; print(yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/ci.yml')))". (Fun fact: PyYAML prints the key on as True, because YAML 1.1 reads bare on as a boolean. GitHub reads it correctly.)

Tip

Debugging a red CI run: read the first meaningful error, find which step/command failed, run that same command locally, fix, push again. "Works on my machine" failures usually mean something was installed on your laptop but not declared in requirements/lockfile.

Drill Which test failed, and what did the code return?

pytest ends with FAILED test_cart.py::test_total_with_tax_adds_15_percent - assert 15.0 == 115.0. Which value came from the code, and how do you rerun only this test? What does echo $? print?

Show answer
$ pytest test_cart.py::test_total_with_tax_adds_15_percent
$ echo $?
1

The test is assert total_with_tax([100]) == 115.0, so the left value 15.0 is what the code returned (it multiplied by 0.15 instead of 1.15). Exit code 1 = tests failed.

Drill Pick the boundary tests

Rule: "free shipping for orders of 50 or more". The code says if total > 50. Existing tests use 10 and 120. Which two totals should you add, and which one fails today?

Show answer
def test_order_of_exactly_50_ships_free():
    assert shipping_cost(50) == 0        # fails today (code says 5)

def test_order_just_under_50_pays():
    assert shipping_cost(49.99) == 5     # passes; guards the other side

Test the last value on each side of the edge. 10 and 120 can't tell > from >=.

Quiz-style Q16 recreation: password length

main has one commit "Add password length check". is_valid_length("abcdefgh") returns False; a password needs at least 8 chars. Make exactly two commits: a test whose message contains "failing test", then the fix. Checked: HEAD~1 test file contains "abcdefgh", HEAD~1 validate.py unchanged, 3 commits, at least 3 tests all passing.

Show answer
$ cat validate.py                 # return len(password) > 8

In the Files panel open test_validate.py, add at the end, save with Ctrl+S:

def test_accepts_exactly_eight_characters():
    assert is_valid_length("abcdefgh") is True
$ pytest                          # 1 failed, 2 passed
$ git add test_validate.py
$ git commit -m "Add failing test for 8-character password"

In the Files panel open validate.py, change > 8 to >= 8, save with Ctrl+S. Then:

$ pytest                          # 3 passed
$ git add validate.py
$ git commit -m "Fix password length check"
$ git log --oneline               # 3 commits

Add the test file only in commit 1, so validate.py at HEAD~1 is still the original.

Quiz-style Duplicate email returns 201

signup(repo, email) returns 201 for a second signup with the same email; it should return 409. Tests use FakeUserRepo, an in-memory fake. Write the failing test and the fix as two commits. Why not use Mock() for the repo?

Show answer

In the Files panel open test_signup.py, add at the end, save with Ctrl+S:

def test_signup_rejects_duplicate_email():
    repo = FakeUserRepo()
    signup(repo, "user@example.com")
    status = signup(repo, "user@example.com")
    assert status == 409
    assert repo.count() == 1
$ pytest ; git add test_signup.py ; git commit -m "Add failing test for duplicate email signup"

In the Files panel open signup.py, add these two lines inside signup, just before repo.save(email), save with Ctrl+S:

    if repo.find(email) is not None:
        return 409
$ pytest ; git add signup.py ; git commit -m "Return 409 for duplicate email"

A Mock stores nothing, so find never sees the first user; you could only assert "save was called". The fake really stores users, so the test checks the behaviour.

Quiz-style Oops, a third commit

You finished Q16 and then ran git add . && git commit -m wip, which committed notes.txt and a debug print. The check needs exactly 3 commits. Remove the wip commit, keep notes.txt on disk (untracked), drop the debug print.

Show answer
$ git reset --soft HEAD~1                  # wip commit gone, its changes staged
$ git restore --staged notes.txt grade.py  # unstage both
$ git restore grade.py                     # throw away the debug print
$ git status                               # Untracked: notes.txt

git revert would add a 5th commit; --hard would delete notes.txt; --amend would fold the junk into your fix commit.

Quiz-style PR merged with failing tests

The workflow has on: push: branches: [main] and the test step has continue-on-error: true. A PR with a failing test showed no checks and was merged. Fix the YAML, and say what else must change outside the file.

Show answer

In the Files panel open .github/workflows/ci.yml, make these parts read as below, save with Ctrl+S:

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
...
      - name: Run unit tests
        run: pytest -q            # continue-on-error removed
$ git add .github/workflows/ci.yml ; git commit -m "Run CI on pull requests to main"

Outside YAML: in GitHub's branch protection / ruleset for main, make the job test a required status check. Trigger + rule = merge gate.

Twist The surviving mutant

Code is return len(pw) >= 8. Tests check "abc" (False) and a 19-char password (True). A mutation tool reports: mutant >= 8 -> > 8 SURVIVED, mutant >= 8 -> >= 7 SURVIVED. Which tests kill them? Does the code need a fix?

Show answer

In the Files panel open the test file, add these two tests, save with Ctrl+S:

def test_accepts_exactly_eight_characters():
    assert is_valid_length("abcdefgh") is True    # kills > 8

def test_rejects_seven_characters():
    assert is_valid_length("abcdefg") is False    # kills >= 7

No code fix: the code is right, the tests were weak. Both new tests pass on the real code and fail on the mutants. Commit only the test file.

Twist Two bugs, four commits

shipping_cost(50) returns 5 (should be free) and is_valid_postcode("3000") returns False (4 digits is valid). The team wants one failing-test + fix pair per bug. What are the four commits and what does pytest say at each?

Show answer
1. test shipping_cost(50) == 0       "Add failing test for free shipping at 50"   pytest: red
2. total > 50  ->  total >= 50       "Fix free shipping at 50"                    pytest: green
3. test is_valid_postcode("3000")   "Add failing test for 4-digit postcode"      pytest: red
4. len(code) == 5  ->  == 4          "Fix postcode length"                        pytest: green

If you write both tests first, commit 2 is still red. If you fix both bugs in commit 2, the postcode test in commit 3 never fails, so it proves nothing.

Quiz-style Hard: which of 8 commits broke it? (git bisect)

Rule: 10 or more items get 10% off. Tag v1.0 was right; 8 commits later, exactly 10 items get no discount, and CI is green (tests only use 3 and 20 items). Find the first bad commit without reading every diff, then do the usual failing-test + fix pair. Where do you put the test while bisecting, and why?

Show answer

In the Files panel click + New file, type test_regression.py (a NEW, untracked file), make it read, save with Ctrl+S:

from pricing import bulk_discount


def test_exactly_ten_items_get_discount():
    assert bulk_discount(10) == 0.10
$ pytest -q                       # 1 failed: bug reproduced
$ git bisect start
$ git bisect bad                  # HEAD is bad
$ git bisect good v1.0            # this one was good; git checks out the middle
$ git bisect run pytest -q test_regression.py
e660570... is the first bad commit
    Tidy pricing code              # qty >= BULK_MIN became qty > BULK_MIN
$ git bisect reset                # back on main (else you stay on a detached HEAD)
$ git add test_regression.py ; git commit -m "Add failing test for exactly 10 items"
# Files panel: open pricing.py, qty > BULK_MIN becomes qty >= BULK_MIN, Ctrl+S
$ pytest -q ; git add pricing.py ; git commit -m "Fix bulk discount for 10 items"

git bisect run reads the exit code: 0 = good, 1 = bad (125 = skip). An untracked test file stays put while bisect checks out old commits; editing a tracked test file first makes the checkout refuse ("local changes would be overwritten"). Binary search: 8 commits need only about 3 test runs. Without run, test each step yourself and type git bisect good or git bisect bad.

Quiz-style Hard: one table of boundary tests (parametrize)

Ticket prices: 0-4 free, 5-17 = 8, 18-64 = 15, 65-120 = 10, below 0 or above 120 = ValueError. Write the fewest test functions that check both sides of every edge and both invalid edges. Which ages, and what does a failing row look like?

Show answer

In the Files panel open test_tickets.py, add this (keep the old tests), save with Ctrl+S:

import pytest
from tickets import ticket_price

@pytest.mark.parametrize("age, expected", [
    (0, 0), (4, 0), (5, 8), (17, 8), (18, 15), (64, 15), (65, 10), (120, 10)])
def test_price(age, expected):
    assert ticket_price(age) == expected

@pytest.mark.parametrize("age", [-1, 121])
def test_invalid_age(age):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        ticket_price(age)
$ pytest -v
test_tickets.py::test_price[65-10] FAILED       # each row is its own test, with its values in the id

The names are ONE string "age, expected"; each tuple is one run. Every edge needs the last value inside AND the first value outside (64 and 65; 120 and 121), or a </<= slip survives.

Twist Hard: green alone, red in CI (flaky tests)

pytest test_cart.py::test_count_after_add passes. The full pytest run fails it. A second test, test_shop_is_open, calls is_open() (which uses datetime.now()) and fails only in the 2 a.m. nightly CI run. Why is each flaky, how do you rerun just the failures quickly, and how do you make both deterministic?

Show answer
$ pytest -x            # stop at the first failure
$ pytest --lf          # rerun only the tests that failed last time (--last-failed)
$ pytest -p no:randomly -v   # (only if pytest-randomly is installed) fixed order

# 1) shared state: cart.py keeps a module-level list, an earlier test left items in it.
@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def empty_cart():
    cart.clear()          # fresh state before EVERY test
    yield

# 2) time: pass the time in instead of reading the real clock
def test_shop_is_open_monday_10am():
    assert is_open(datetime(2026, 10, 26, 10, 0)) is True
def test_shop_is_closed_at_2am():
    assert is_open(datetime(2026, 10, 26, 2, 0)) is False

A test must give the same result alone, in any order, at any time. Order-dependence = shared state between tests (fix: a fixture that resets it, or a new object per test). Time-dependence = the real clock (fix: inject the time / a fake clock). Never "fix" flakiness with retries, skip or continue-on-error: that hides real bugs.

Twist Hard: write the whole CI workflow, with a lint gate

No CI exists. Write .github/workflows/ci.yml: on pull requests to main and pushes to main, job build-and-test must install, lint with ruff check ., then run pytest. requirements.txt only lists pytest, and ruff check . currently reports 2 errors. What do you commit, in which order?

Show answer
$ ruff check .                 # read the 2 errors

In the Files panel fix both errors in temps.py, then open requirements.txt and add a line ruff (the runner is a clean VM: no ruff unless installed). Save each with Ctrl+S.

$ ruff check . ; pytest -q
$ git add temps.py requirements.txt ; git commit -m "Fix lint errors, add ruff"

Click + New file, type .github/workflows/ci.yml (the folders are created for you), make it read, save with Ctrl+S:

name: CI
on:
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
  push:
    branches: [main]
jobs:
  build-and-test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: "3.12"
      - run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - run: ruff check .         # exit 1 on findings = red job
      - run: pytest
$ python3 -c "import yaml; print(yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/ci.yml')))"
$ git add .github/workflows/ci.yml ; git commit -m "Add CI workflow"

Lint fix first, so the workflow's first run is green. No continue-on-error and no || true: either makes a failing step count as success. Missing ruff in requirements = "ruff: command not found" (exit 127) on CI even though it works on your laptop. ruff check . --fix removes an unused import, but not an unused variable (an "unsafe" fix): edit that yourself.

Practice labs

Cheat sheet

  • pytest / pytest -q / pytest -v run all tests (normal / quiet / one line each)
  • pytest -x stop at the first failure; pytest -s show print output
  • pytest -k "email and not long" run tests whose name matches (quote it)
  • pytest file.py::test_name run one test
  • echo $? right after: 0 pass, 1 tests failed, 4 bad path/usage, 5 no tests collected
  • assert f(x) == expected a test needs an assert; no assert = always green
  • with pytest.raises(ValueError): f(-1) expect an error (import pytest)
  • Boundary for "at least N": test N-1 and N (plus a normal and an invalid value)
  • git add test_x.py && git commit -m "Add failing test for ..." commit 1: test only, red
  • git add x.py && git commit -m "Fix ..." commit 2: fix only, green
  • git show --stat HEAD~1 / git show HEAD~1:file inspect the test commit like the grader
  • git reset --soft HEAD~1 undo last commit, keep changes staged
  • git restore --staged f unstage; git restore f discard edits
  • git commit --amend -m "msg" rewrite the LAST commit (only before pushing)
  • Coverage = lines that ran, not lines that were checked. pytest --cov=. --cov-report=term-missing (needs pytest-cov)
  • Stub = canned value; fake = working in-memory version; mock = records calls
  • on: pull_request: branches: [main] run CI on PRs into main
  • Required status checks = GitHub branch protection setting, not YAML; remove continue-on-error: true
Topic 6

Database migrations with Flyway, and migration files in git

Write numbered migration files, apply them with flyway migrate, read the history table through flyway info, and recover from checksum mismatches, hand-run SQL and version collisions.

Mental model

Files in git, memory in the database. A migration is a file of SQL (03_add_currency.up.sql). Saving it changes nothing. The runner (Flyway) connects to the database, reads the history table (flyway_schema_history, one row per applied migration), runs every file that has no row yet, in version order, and inserts a row for each. The history is a log: append-only (never edit an applied file), ordered (03 assumes 01 and 02 ran), checksum-verified (edit an applied file and Flyway stops). Anything done by hand is invisible to it.

Setup: the flyway command (Docker) and the Files panel

Flyway is not installed on your laptop. labs/bin/flyway is a tiny script that runs the official Flyway image (flyway/flyway:13.10.0, already pulled) in Docker. It mounts the current folder, runs as your user (so shop.db is not owned by root), and Flyway reads flyway.toml from that folder. So: always cd work first. Once per terminal, from the Practice-Labs folder:

$ export PATH="$PWD/labs/bin:$PATH"
$ cd labs/06-migrations/02-first-migrate && ./setup.sh && cd work
$ flyway info
docker run --rm -u "$(id -u):$(id -g)" -v "$PWD:/work" -w /work flyway/flyway:13.10.0 migrate
  1. run a throw-away container (removed when it exits)
  2. as your user id, so created files belong to you
  3. mount this folder at /work and run there: Flyway sees flyway.toml, migrations/, shop.db
  4. the pinned official image
  5. your Flyway command (anything you type after flyway)

Every lab's flyway.toml looks like this. The url says which database (an SQLite file next to it), locations where the files are:

[environments.default]
url = "jdbc:sqlite:shop.db"

[flyway]
environment = "default"
locations = ["filesystem:migrations"]
sqlMigrationPrefix = ""            # course naming: no V in front
sqlMigrationSeparator = "_"        # 03_add_currency -> version 03, description "add currency"
sqlMigrationSuffixes = [".up.sql"] # only .up.sql are migrations; .down.sql are ignored
validateMigrationNaming = true     # misnamed file = error, not silently skipped

Your tools. Like in the quiz, you only write files in migrations/ and run flyway (plus git). shop.db is the database. You never open it yourself: flyway info is your window into it. Write files in the lab tool's Files panel: click + New file, type the path with the folder (migrations/03_add_currency.up.sql), write the SQL, save with Ctrl+S. Click an existing file to open and edit it. Use the terminal for flyway, git, ls, mv, and cat to read a file.

Trap

Flyway reads the file on disk, not your editor tab. Save with Ctrl+S before flyway migrate. Typing only 03_add_currency.up.sql in + New file puts it next to flyway.toml, where Flyway never looks.

Tip

The quiz terminal may have Flyway installed directly. Same commands, same output: flyway migrate, flyway info. The wrapper only hides the Docker part.

File names: course naming vs Flyway's default

NamingExampleUsed in
Course (configured above)03_add_currency.up.sql + 03_add_currency.down.sqlquiz 2 Q18, labs 01, 03, 04, 06-11, 13, 14
Flyway defaultV3__add_currency.sql (capital V, two underscores)Flyway docs, labs 02, 12
Timestamp version202610071517_add_search_index.sqlquiz 2 Q17, labs 05, 10

Version = everything before the first separator. Versions compare as numbers: 3 and 03 are the same version (duplicate!), and 05 < 202610261030. The description is the rest, with _ shown as spaces. Flyway (free edition) has no undo command: it never runs a .down.sql. You write it so the change can be undone later (by a tool or a person); it does not show up in flyway info.

Trap

A file that does not match the naming is not a migration. Without validateMigrationNaming Flyway silently skips it. Rule: if flyway info does not list your file, its name is wrong.

Reading flyway info: the history table for you

flyway info puts two lists side by side: the migration files, and the rows of the history table flyway_schema_history (one row per migration Flyway ran: version, script name, checksum, when, success). It is read-only, so run it first and last, every time.

$ flyway info
Schema version: 05
+-----------+---------+-----------------+----------+---------------------+--------------------+
| Category  | Version | Description     | Type     | Installed On        | State              |
+-----------+---------+-----------------+----------+---------------------+--------------------+
| Versioned | 01      | create users    | SQL      |                     | Ignored (Baseline) |
|           | 01      | existing schema | BASELINE | 2026-10-27 17:24:07 | Baseline           |
| Versioned | 02      | create orders   | SQL      | 2026-10-27 17:24:17 | Success            |
| Versioned | 03      | add city        | SQL      | 2026-10-27 17:24:17 | Success            |
| Versioned | 05      | create coupons  | SQL      | 2026-10-27 17:24:17 | Success            |
| Versioned | 04      | index orders    | SQL      | 2026-10-27 17:24:26 | Out of Order       |
+-----------+---------+-----------------+----------+---------------------+--------------------+
What you seeWhat it means
Schema version: 05highest version that is applied. Pending files don't count.
row orderapplied rows come in the order Flyway ran them (here 04 ran last), then pending ones
Installed Onwhen it ran. Empty = it never ran here.
Successfile has a history row: applied
Pendingfile has no history row yet: the next migrate runs it
Failedit ran without a transaction and broke halfway. Blocks every migrate until flyway repair
Baseline (Type BASELINE)a marker row from flyway baseline: "everything up to here was already there"
Ignored (Baseline) / Below Baselinea file at or below the baseline: its SQL never ran here, and never will
Ignoreda lower version that showed up after a higher one ran. migrate refuses until you allow -outOfOrder=true
Out of Orderapplied, but after a higher version (with -outOfOrder=true)
Category Repeatable, empty Versionan R__ file. Outdated = file changed since it ran (will re-run); Superseded = an old run of it
Trap

"Last one run" and "schema version" are different questions. Above, 04 ran last, but the schema version is 05. If your new file is not in the table at all, its name is wrong (or it is not in migrations/).

The Flyway commands

CommandWhat it does
flyway infotable of every migration: Success / Pending / Baseline / Out of Order. Read-only. Run it first and last.
flyway migratevalidate, then run every pending migration in version order, each in its own transaction, and record a history row
flyway migrate -target=03apply only up to version 03 (staged rollouts)
flyway validatecompare files with history (checksums, missing, pending). Fails also when something is still pending.
flyway repairrewrite stored checksums to match the files, remove failed rows. Does NOT run SQL.
flyway baseline -baselineVersion=02existing DB without history: "already at 02". Creates the history table with a BASELINE row.
flyway migrate -outOfOrder=trueallow a lower version that arrived after a higher one was applied
flyway migrate -skipExecutingMigrations=true -target=03record 03 as applied without running it (only when the same SQL was already run by hand)
$ flyway info
+-----------+---------+----------------+------+---------------------+---------+----------+
| Category  | Version | Description    | Type | Installed On        | State   | Undoable |
+-----------+---------+----------------+------+---------------------+---------+----------+
| Versioned | 01      | create orders  | SQL  | 2026-10-26 16:46:13 | Success | No       |
| Versioned | 02      | index orders   | SQL  | 2026-10-26 16:46:13 | Success | No       |
| Versioned | 03      | add currency   | SQL  |                     | Pending | No       |
| Versioned | 04      | create coupons | SQL  |                     | Pending | No       |
+-----------+---------+----------------+------+---------------------+---------+----------+
$ flyway migrate
Database: jdbc:sqlite:******** (SQLite 3.50)
Successfully validated 4 migrations (execution time 00:00.023s)
Current version of schema "main": 02
Migrating schema "main" to version "03 - add currency"
Migrating schema "main" to version "04 - create coupons"
Successfully applied 2 migrations to schema "main", now at version v04
$ flyway migrate
Schema "main" is up to date. No migration necessary.
Why

What migrate does per file, as on the slide: BEGIN; run the file's SQL; INSERT the history row; COMMIT;. Both commit together or not at all. On SQLite and PostgreSQL DDL is transactional, so a failing migration prints Changes successfully rolled back and leaves no history row: fix the file and run migrate again.

BEGIN / COMMIT / ROLLBACK, and reverse migrations

What it isExample result
ROLLBACKdiscards changes that have not committedBEGIN; ALTER TABLE users DROP COLUMN bio; ROLLBACK; bio values still there
Reverse (down) migrationnew SQL run after the up committedALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN bio text; bio is back, NULL in every row
Restorerecover data from a backupvalues as of the backup time

Without BEGIN each statement autocommits: if statement 2 fails, statement 1 stays. A down migration cannot restore values the up deleted (quiz 2 Q9: answer NULL for every row). Downs undo in reverse order (04 then 03), and each down undoes only its own migration. Flyway free edition never runs a down. On a shared database the way back is to roll forward: put the reverse SQL in the next migration.

Files panel, + New file migrations/03_restore_bio.up.sql, write, Ctrl+S:

ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN bio TEXT;
$ flyway migrate     # 03 runs: bio is back, NULL in every row
$ flyway info        # 01, 02, 03 Success: the history tells exactly what happened

Errors you must recognise

ERROR: Validate failed: Migrations have failed validation
Migration checksum mismatch for migration version 02
-> Applied to database : 1944033762
-> Resolved locally    : 2087934152
Either revert the changes to the migration, or run repair to update the schema history.

Means: someone edited an already-applied file. Flyway stops before running anything.

Fix: put the file back (git restore --source=HEAD~1 file or git revert) and put the change in the next migration (03). repair only makes the error go away: the new SQL never runs, and other databases differ. Use repair only for harmless edits (comments) or to clear a failed row.

ERROR: Migration of schema "main" to version "03 - add full name" failed! Changes successfully rolled back.
Message    : [SQLITE_ERROR] SQL error or missing database (duplicate column name: full_name)

Means: the change was already made by hand, outside Flyway. Hand-run SQL changes the schema but writes no history row, so 03 is still Pending (quiz 2 Q8). PostgreSQL says column "full_name" of relation "users" already exists; for a table: table x already exists.

Fix: if the hand-run SQL is exactly the file's SQL, record 03 without running it: flyway migrate -skipExecutingMigrations=true -target=03, then a plain flyway migrate for the rest. -target=03 matters: without it every pending migration is recorded but never run.

ERROR: Validate failed: Migrations have failed validation
Detected failed migration to version 02 (create coupons).
Please remove any half-completed changes then run repair to fix the schema history.

Means: 02 ran without a transaction (e.g. executeInTransaction=false, or MySQL DDL) and broke halfway. The first statements stayed; flyway info shows 02 Failed.

Fix: fix the file (allowed: it never succeeded) and make it safe to re-run (IF NOT EXISTS, INSERT OR IGNORE), then flyway repair (removes the Failed row) and flyway migrate.

ERROR: Found more than one migration with version 04
Offenders:
-> /work/migrations/04_add_coupons.sql (SQL)
-> /work/migrations/04_add_search_index.sql (SQL)

Means: two branches picked the same number. Git merged fine (different file names); only the runner complains.

Fix: on your branch, git mv your file to a timestamp version, commit, merge main in. Never renumber the one already on main.

ERROR: Found non-empty schema(s) "main" but no schema history table. Use baseline() or set baselineOnMigrate to true ...

Means: the database has tables but was never managed by Flyway.

Fix: flyway baseline -baselineVersion=02 (the version the DB already matches), then flyway migrate.

Detected resolved migration not applied to database: 05.
To ignore this migration, set -ignoreMigrationPatterns='*:ignored'. To allow executing this migration, set -outOfOrder=true.

Means: a lower version showed up after a higher one was applied (e.g. 202610261030 applied, then 05 merged in). The same text without the outOfOrder part comes from flyway validate when something is simply Pending: run migrate.

Fix: flyway migrate -outOfOrder=true.

ERROR: Invalid SQL filenames found:
Invalid versioned migration name format: 04-add-x.up.sql (could not recognise version number 04-add-x)

Means: wrong separator/prefix (here - instead of _; in default naming V3_x.sql with one underscore).

Fix: rename the file.

ERROR: Migration of schema "main" to version "03 - add currency" failed! Changes successfully rolled back.
Message    : [SQLITE_ERROR] SQL error or missing database (Cannot add a NOT NULL column with default value NULL)

Means: SQLite can't add a NOT NULL column to rows that exist without a default.

Fix: the migration rolled back and has no history row, so just fix the file: ADD COLUMN currency TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'INR' (old rows get INR), save, flyway migrate again.

Migrations in git: timestamp versions

Sequence numbers collide when two people write migration 04 the same afternoon. Timestamps (yyyyMMddHHmm) are unique without coordination and sort by when they were written.

git mv migrations/04_add_search_index.sql migrations/202610071517_add_search_index.sql
  1. rename AND stage it (delete + add in one go)
  2. old name (your branch's migration only)
  3. new name: timestamp version, same description
$ git branch --show-current
search
$ git mv migrations/04_add_search_index.sql migrations/202610071517_add_search_index.sql
$ git commit -m "Rename search index migration to timestamp version"
$ git merge main --no-edit          # without --no-edit an editor opens: vim :wq, nano Ctrl+O Enter Ctrl+X
$ ls migrations
01_create_users.sql  02_add_billing.sql  03_add_full_name.sql  04_add_coupons.sql  202610071517_add_search_index.sql
Trap

mv + git commit -am commits the delete but not the new (untracked) file: your migration vanishes from the commit. Use git mv, or git add -A. Rename the .down.sql too when there is one.

Tip

shop.db is usually in .gitignore: git holds the files (the recipe), each database holds its own history (what was cooked).

Expand / backfill / transition / contract

Renaming user_name to full_name while old and new app instances run side by side (quiz 2 Q11):

StepSQL / appAs a migration
1 ExpandALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN full_name text; then app writes both02_add_full_name.up.sql, down drops it
2 BackfillUPDATE users SET full_name = user_name WHERE full_name IS NULL; (in batches)a job, or 03_backfill_full_name.up.sql
3 Transitionapp reads full_name, still writes bothno SQL
4 ContractALTER TABLE users DROP COLUMN user_name;04_drop_user_name.up.sql; a good down re-adds AND copies back
Trap

A single RENAME COLUMN breaks every old instance at once. Backfilling before the app writes both leaves NULLs for rows written meanwhile (Q11 option B).

Drill Which rows are in the history table after this?

Files 01, 02, 03 exist. 01 and 02 were applied by Flyway. A teammate runs 03's SQL by hand, outside Flyway. What does flyway info show for 03, and what does flyway migrate do?

Show answer
03 Pending (no history row). migrate runs 03 again -> "duplicate column name" / "already exists", rolled back.

The history table only changes when the runner records a migration.

Drill Add a column the Flyway way (default naming)

migrations/V1__create_users.sql is applied. Add email TEXT to users.

Show answer

Files panel: + New file migrations/V2__add_email.sql, write, Ctrl+S:

ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN email TEXT;
$ flyway migrate
$ flyway info

New change = new file with the next version. Capital V, two underscores.

Quiz-style Q17: rename to a timestamp and merge main

On search, which adds 04_add_search_index.sql; main has 04_add_coupons.sql. Give yours version 202610071517, bring main into search, commit. Don't push; leave coupons alone.

Show answer
$ git mv migrations/04_add_search_index.sql migrations/202610071517_add_search_index.sql
$ git commit -m "Use timestamp version for search index migration"
$ git merge main --no-edit

Checked: the new file at search contains CREATE INDEX, the 04 search file is gone, 04_add_coupons.sql exists, main is merged into search.

Quiz-style Q18 (reconstructed): two migrations + two reverse migrations

shop.db has 01 (create orders) and 02 (index) applied, 3 orders. Write 03 adding currency TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'INR', 04 creating coupons(code TEXT PRIMARY KEY, percent INTEGER NOT NULL), their down files, and apply with flyway.

Show answer

Four files, each with + New file in the Files panel and Ctrl+S:

migrations/03_add_currency.up.sql      ALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN currency TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'INR';
migrations/03_add_currency.down.sql    ALTER TABLE orders DROP COLUMN currency;
migrations/04_create_coupons.up.sql    CREATE TABLE coupons (code TEXT PRIMARY KEY, percent INTEGER NOT NULL);
migrations/04_create_coupons.down.sql  DROP TABLE coupons;
$ flyway info        # 03, 04 Pending (down files are never listed)
$ flyway migrate
$ flyway info        # 01-04 Success

Without DEFAULT 'INR' SQLite refuses a NOT NULL column on a table with rows. Downs undo newest first: 04, then 03.

Quiz-style A teammate edited applied migration 02

flyway migrate prints "checksum mismatch for migration version 02". The edit (last commit) added billing_email to 02. Fix it without repair and without rewriting history.

Show answer
$ git restore --source=HEAD~1 migrations/02_add_billing.up.sql migrations/02_add_billing.down.sql
# Files panel, + New file, Ctrl+S:
#   migrations/03_add_billing_email.up.sql    ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN billing_email TEXT;
#   migrations/03_add_billing_email.down.sql  ALTER TABLE users DROP COLUMN billing_email;
$ flyway migrate
$ git add -A && git commit -m "Move billing_email into migration 03"

Treat an applied migration as immutable history; fix it with the next one.

Twist Your local DB already ran a timestamp migration, then main brings 05

You applied 202610261030_create_reviews locally before merging main, which adds 05_create_wishlist. flyway migrate refuses. Why, and what do you run?

Show answer
Detected resolved migration not applied to database: 05 ... set -outOfOrder=true
$ flyway migrate -outOfOrder=true      # info then shows 05 as "Out of Order"

Flyway applies in version order; a lower version arriving later is out of order and needs explicit permission.

Twist Down for a contract migration

04_drop_user_name.up.sql drops user_name after full_name was backfilled. Write a down that gives back a useful user_name.

Show answer
ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN user_name text;
UPDATE users SET user_name = full_name;

ADD COLUMN alone gives NULLs. Copying works only because full_name still has the values; data that exists nowhere else needs a backup.

Quiz-style flyway info says 02 Failed

02_create_coupons.up.sql = CREATE TABLE coupons, INSERT two coupons, CREATE INDEX. It ran without a transaction and failed on the index (typo coupns). flyway migrate now says Detected failed migration to version 02. Please remove any half-completed changes then run repair. Get 02 fully applied, using only the editor and flyway.

Show answer

Open migrations/02_create_coupons.up.sql in the Files panel, make every step re-runnable and fix the typo, Ctrl+S:

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS coupons (code TEXT PRIMARY KEY, percent INTEGER NOT NULL);
INSERT OR IGNORE INTO coupons (code, percent) VALUES ('WELCOME10', 10), ('FESTIVE20', 20);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_coupons_percent ON coupons (percent);
$ flyway repair       # removes the Failed row; runs no SQL
$ flyway migrate      # 02 runs from line 1: table + rows skipped, index created. Success

Statements 1 and 2 committed before the failure, so a plain re-run fails with table coupons already exists (and a new Failed row). Editing 02 is fine here: it never succeeded anywhere. Don't delete the CREATE/INSERT from 02: a fresh database still needs them. With a transaction (the SQLite/PostgreSQL default) there is no Failed row at all: everything rolls back.

Quiz-style Change a view: new V file or edit the R file?

Default naming. R__active_users.sql (DROP VIEW IF EXISTS + CREATE VIEW) is applied. Users need a city column, and the view must show it. What files do you write, what does flyway info show before migrate, and what is in the history table afterwards?

Show answer

Files panel: + New file migrations/V3__add_city.sql, write, Ctrl+S:

ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN city TEXT;

Open migrations/R__active_users.sql, change the SELECT line, Ctrl+S:

  SELECT id, name, city FROM users WHERE active = 1;
$ flyway info       # V3 Pending; old R row "Outdated" + new R row "Pending"
$ flyway migrate    # V3 first, then the repeatable

Versioned (V) = runs once, never edit. Repeatable (R__, no version) = re-runs whenever its checksum changes, after all versioned ones; editing it IS how you change it. History gets a second R__active_users.sql row (version empty, new checksum); info shows the old one as Superseded.

Twist Two lower versions arrive late: outOfOrder or renumber?

Your DB is at 202610251200. Now there are 202610221500_add_order_status (teammate's hotfix, already applied in production) and your own 202610231000_add_order_notes (never committed, never applied anywhere). flyway info shows both as Ignored. Fix it.

Show answer
$ mv migrations/202610231000_add_order_notes.up.sql   migrations/202610271100_add_order_notes.up.sql
$ mv migrations/202610231000_add_order_notes.down.sql migrations/202610271100_add_order_notes.down.sql
$ git add migrations && git commit -m "Add order notes migration (202610271100)"
$ flyway migrate -outOfOrder=true     # hotfix: "Out of Order"; notes: normal Success

Renumber only what ran nowhere (the hotfix's version is in production's history; renaming it would make production run it twice). Rename before outOfOrder, or the old version gets applied too. git mv fails on untracked files (not under version control): use mv + git add.

Twist Read flyway info: last run vs current version

flyway info lists, in run order: the 01 file Ignored (Baseline) + a BASELINE row 01, then 02, 03, 05 Success, then 04 Out of Order. Which version ran last? What is the schema version? Which file's SQL never ran here? Then someone adds only a -- comment to applied 03 and validate fails: repair or new migration?

Show answer
$ flyway info                              # last row, latest Installed On: 04 (Out of Order)
$ flyway info | grep "Schema version"      # 05
# never executed here: 01 (Ignored (Baseline): the BASELINE row stands in for it)
$ git diff HEAD~1 HEAD                     # only a comment line
$ flyway repair                            # records the new checksum; runs no SQL

Row order in info = order Flyway ran things; version = order it wants them. The checksum covers the whole text, comments too. A comment changes nothing in any database, so repair is right; an edit that changes SQL (lab 07) needs a new migration instead.

Practice labs

Cheat sheet

  • export PATH="$PWD/labs/bin:$PATH" once per terminal from the Practice-Labs folder, then cd work
  • flyway info what is applied / pending (start and end with it)
  • flyway migrate run pending migrations in order, record history rows
  • flyway migrate -target=03 stop at version 03
  • flyway validate files vs history (checksums); fails if something is pending
  • flyway repair accept edited checksums / drop failed rows. Runs no SQL. Last resort.
  • flyway baseline -baselineVersion=02 adopt an existing DB
  • flyway migrate -outOfOrder=true apply a lower version that arrived late
  • 03_add_x.up.sql / 03_add_x.down.sql course naming; V3__add_x.sql Flyway default
  • flyway migrate -skipExecutingMigrations=true -target=03 record 03 without running it (SQL was run by hand, identical)
  • Files panel: + New file migrations/03_x.up.sql, write, Ctrl+S (save before you migrate)
  • info states: Success, Pending, Failed (repair), Baseline / Ignored (Baseline), Ignored + Out of Order, Outdated / Superseded (R__)
  • No undo in Flyway free: roll forward with the next migration; still write a .down.sql for each
  • Never edit an applied migration: write the next one
  • Hand-run SQL = schema changed, no history row = runner fails with "already exists"
  • Down of DROP COLUMN = column back, all NULL. Only a backup restores values.
  • git mv old new + commit, then git merge main --no-edit (timestamp rename)
  • Rename: expand -> backfill -> transition -> contract
Topic 7

Hosting, restart policies, health checks and module boundaries

Run a service the way the T4L2 demo does, pick the right restart policy, write health checks that don't cause restart storms, make an app safe to scale out, and fix a module-boundary violation (T4L1).

Mental model

Three questions: where does it run (VM, container PaaS, managed containers, serverless), who keeps it running, and why that choice. "Who keeps it running" is a ladder: systemd = one process.
